a bit more consistent.\n\t\t// See https://github.com/INRIA/spoon/issues/4758 for more details.\n\t\tif (!child.isImplicit() || !ctClass.isEnum() || !constructor.getParameters().isEmpty()) setTargetFromUnqualifiedAccess(CtInvocation<T> invocation) {\n\t\t// A call to a statically imported method (e.g. assertTrue(false)) is modelled as\n\t\t// \"this.assertTrue(false)\" by JDT. We need to unscramble that heuristically and replace the\n\t\t// \"this\" reference with the correct type (e.g. org.junit.api.Assertions)\n\n\t\t// Additionally, references to methods of enclosing classes are also modelled as \"this\" by JDT.\n\t\t// Compare with Test \"correctlySetsThisTargetForUnqualifiedCalls\".\n\n\t\t// We need a MessageSend as the parent to resolve the actualType from the receiver\n\t\tif (!(parentPair.node() instanceof MessageSend messageSend)) {\n\t\t\treturn false;\n\t\t}\n\t\tif (messageSend.actualReceiverType == null || messageSend.receiver.resolvedType == null) {\n\t\t\treturn false;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tReferenceBuilder referenceBuilder = jdtTreeBuilder.getReferencesBuilder();\n\t\tCtTypeReference<?> actualReceiverType = referenceBuilder.getTypeReference(messageSend.actualReceiverType);\n\t\tCtTypeReference<?> resolvedReceiverType = referenceBuilder.getTypeReference(messageSend.receiver.resolvedType);\n\n\t\t// If they match we have a normal \"this\" reference\n\t\tif (actualReceiverType.equals(resolvedReceiverType)) {\n\t\t\treturn false;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif (messageSend.binding() == null || !messageSend.binding().isStatic()) {\n\t\t\t// Emulate outer this access\n\t\t\twhile (resolvedReceiverType != null) {\n\t\t\t\tresolvedReceiverType = resolvedReceiverType.getDeclaringType();\n\t\t\t\tif (actualReceiverType.equals(resolvedReceiverType)) {\n\t\t\t\t\tinvocation.setTarget(jdtTreeBuilder.getFactory().Code().createThisAccess(actualReceiverType, true));\n\t\t\t\t\treturn true;\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t// I don't think this can happen but let's be conservative and preserve the previous behaviour\n\t\t\treturn false;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// If not, we probably had a static import/static outer method reference here and should use the actual type\n\t\t// instead\n\t\tinvocation.setTarget(jdtTreeBuilder.getFactory().Code().createTypeAccess(actualReceiverType, true));\n\t\treturn true;\n\t}\n\n\tprivate <T> boolean packageRef.sourceStart(), firstComment);\n\t\t\t\t\tdeclStart = PositionBuilder.findNextNonWhitespace(false, content, packageRef.sourceStart(), commentEnd + 1);\n\t\t\t\t} else {\n\t\t\t\t\tdeclStart {"type":"assistant","message":{"content":[{"text":"The dependency upgrade is complete and verified. Here's a summary of what I did.\n\n## Summary\n\nI upgraded `org.eclipse.jdt:org.eclipse.jdt.core` from **3.39.0 → 3.40.0** in `pom.xml`, then fixed two breakages in the JDT AST-to-Spoon tree builder caused by internal API changes in the new version.\n\n### Changes\n\n**1. `pom.xml`** — bumped the version to `3.40.0`.\n\n**2. `ParentExiter.java`** (compile break) — JDT renamed the field `CaseStatement.isExpr` to `isSwitchRule`. Updated the case-kind detection accordingly (both indicate an arrow-style switch rule vs. a colon case).\n\n**3. `JDTTreeBuilder.java`** (behavioral break) — the trickier issue. In 3.40.0, `SwitchExpression` no longer overrides `traverse()`, so it inherits `SwitchStatement#traverse`, which calls `visitor.visit(this, scope)` with the **static type `SwitchStatement`**. As a result, a `SwitchExpression` AST node now dispatches to Spoon's `visit(SwitchStatement, …)` overload instead of `visit(SwitchExpression, …)`. Spoon then built a statement-`CtSwitch` where an expression-`CtSwitchExpression` was needed — and because a statement isn't a `CtExpression`, entire constructs like `int x = switch(i) {...}` were silently dropped from the model.\n\nI fixed this by delegating in both `visit(SwitchStatement, …)` and `endVisit(SwitchStatement, …)`: when the node is actually a `SwitchExpression`, forward to the corresponding switch-expression handler.\n\n### Verification\n- `mvn compile` and `mvn test-compile` succeed.\n- `SwitchCaseTest` (the directly-affected suite): **21/21 pass** (was 2 failures + 3 errors before the fix).\n- Broader related suites all pass: `SwitchPatternTest`, `RecordPatternTest`, `TypePatternTest`, `CtCaseTest`, `PositionTest`, plus the full `spoon.test.model.**` and `spoon.test.compilation.**` packages.\n\nI diagnosed the root cause by disassembling the ecj 3.39.0 vs 3.40.0 sources and reproducing the exact AST-dispatch difference with a standalone parser trace, so the fix targets the real cause rather than a symptom.
